# MEKA OS build plan (the build board)

This file is the single source of truth for what is built, what is next and what is blocked. Unattended build runs
read it first and update it last. The owner reads it to see progress.

Status marks: `[x]` done · `[~]` in progress (partly landed) · `[ ]` not started · `[!]` blocked on the owner (see "Needs Meka").

## Rules every build run follows

1. **Hard constraints (never break, never "temporarily" relax):**
   - MEKA OS never holds trading or exchange credentials. Trading is event/approval based only.
   - Personal message sending is never more than "ask me, then send" (autonomy Level 3).
   - No unofficial WhatsApp client libraries. Messaging goes through the Android notification listener only.
   - Financial actions and trading approvals always need biometric confirmation.
   - Untrusted content (email, messages, web, news) never raises its own autonomy level (ADR-006).
2. **One item per run.** Take the first unchecked, unblocked item below. If it is too big for one run, land a
   working slice, mark it `[~]` and write what remains under the item.
3. **Main stays green.** Run `tools/core-verify.sh` and the relevant Gradle tests locally where possible, push, then
   watch CI. Pushes to `main` deploy the backend after CI passes. If CI fails, fix it in the same run or revert.
4. **Database migrations are additive only** (new tables/columns). Never drop or rewrite the owner's data.
5. **No new paid services, no new AWS resources that cost more than ~£2/month, no new third-party data sharing**
   without the owner's yes. Put the question under "Needs Meka" and skip to the next item.
6. **Animation is part of done.** Every new screen follows the motion catalogue (docs/motion-catalogue.md) and honours reduced motion.
7. **Both apps.** A feature is done when it works on the Fold and the Mac (Mac may use a simpler layout).
8. **Dark is the default theme**; every screen must look right in Dark and Light.
9. **Commit messages** start with the milestone (`M1:`, `V1:`, `V2:`). Update this file in the same commit.
10. **Actions minutes are finite** (2,000 free standard minutes a month, then Meka's Actions budget, raised on
    2026-10-06 to about $40/month so the build can run around the clock; it stops at the limit). Docs-only commits
    don't trigger CI, and deploy only runs when a commit touches the server (backend/, infra/, core/, build files).
    **No daily push cap** (Meka removed it 2026-10-09 20:23: "happy with cost, just need to get this completed sooner";
    the GitHub budget limit still stops runs if it's ever reached). Keep one coherent slice per push. Runs go **back to back** (Meka, 2026-10-07): a run that landed
    something with CI green starts the next one with fire_trigger; the hourly schedule is the safety net. The `macos` job
    (about 10x the cost of Linux) runs weekly and on manual dispatch only, and build runs dispatch it **at most once a
    day, at night**: the first run at or after 21:00 UTC dispatches `ci` once if any commit since the last green
    dispatched/scheduled `ci` run touched `macos/`, `core/` or the build files. A Mac-touching item can be marked done
    before then ("Mac checked nightly"); if the nightly run is red, the next run fixes it first. If CI jobs sit queued
    with no runner, the allowance is used up: stop without pushing code; it resets on the 1st.
11. **Stop rule.** If something can't be undone and could reasonably go either way, don't do it: write it under
    "Needs Meka" and move on.

**Type weight (Meka, 2026-10-06):** calendar events and other context you don't act on use the regular `body` style;
the heavier `itemTitle` weight is for things you act on (tasks, habits, approvals). Meka found medium-weight event titles
too heavy on the Fold. **Fold review 2026-10-08:** task rows (Today, Needs you, the Calendar agenda) and booked-session rows
use `body` too; Up next's title is the one bold line.

12. **Keep this board lean** (Meka, 2026-10-11: usage limits). Each open item is its description plus at most one *What remains* line and its *Check on* lines. When a slice lands, write its detailed history (what changed, files, tests) in docs/build-plan-archive.md under the item's name, not here; when an item is done, move the whole item to the archive. Read docs/motion-catalogue.md only when the slice changes UI, the archive only when an open item points back to a finished one.

13. **No personal details in this repository — it is public** (Meka, 2026-10-12 20:33: "stay public but move personal information into my data"). Never write into code, tests, fixtures, docs, commit messages or CI logs: family members' names, the children's names or school, home or work address/postcode/town, phone numbers, email addresses, account ids that identify him, or anything he tells you about his life. In the board use roles ("his wife", "the younger boy (U7)", "the older boy (U10)", "the boys' school", "home", "work"). Anything the app needs to know about him lives in his MEKA data (synced, encrypted) and is entered on the device or by an onboarding step — the code holds only generic defaults. Tests use invented names ("Alex", "Sam", "Oakfield School", "AB1 2CD").

## Motion catalogue

Moved to **docs/motion-catalogue.md** (2026-10-11). Read it before building or changing any screen; it applies to every screen exactly as before.

## Daily plan (Meka, 2026-10-14 10:30: "a daily plan of what will be built so I can plan and track it")

Runs keep to this order after any new "Take first" fix from Meka. It mirrors the Navilife tab of his project tracker (https://claude.ai/artifact — see the midnight report task), which the midnight run updates. Items marked [!] for mail access are **buildable now** (Connect mail and Inbox autopilot landed 2026-10-13): build them, and say in the run summary that they need his accounts connected to test. If a day's items finish early, start the next day's.
- **Mon 26 Oct (set by Claude 2026-10-25 22:30, mirrored on the tracker):** finish instant taps · one voice for Obi in Live · confirm Clean up / Mark read run for Personal Gmail.
- **Tue 27 Oct:** calmer Today (approved mockup, Done today choice) · Money bugs (A).
- **Wed 28 Oct:** Money redesign part 1: tabs, Spending, Bills, Accounts at all three sizes.
- **Thu 29 Oct:** Money part 2: Plan (what-if saving, clear my cards, goals), Obi's money check, Ask Obi.
- **Fri 30 Oct:** Money Trading tab · Week board alignment on the open Fold.
- **Sat 31 Oct:** folded Week board (approved 27 Oct) · screenshot checks against every mockup · buffer.
- **Wed 14 Oct:** natural voice (finish) · school dates for both boys + School rhythm · Ask's help: Take me there · two cars (Car on autopilot per car) · Car and parking (Where I parked, Find my car, big car parks).
- **Thu 15 Oct:** Voice notes into actions · Screenshots and photos that do something + Photo of a letter → task · Weekly reflection · Kids' admin · Barça match day.
- **Fri 16 Oct:** the email assistant set: Email triage completeness · Promises → Waiting for, receipts → Vault, renewals radar · Follow-up radar · Scheduling assistant · Meeting prep · School and family logistics from email · Life audit · Returns and warranties completeness.
- **Sat 17 Oct:** Money manager slices 1–4 (connect bank, transactions and categories, Money screen, nudges); if Enable Banking isn't set up yet, build the CSV-import path and the screens with invented data first.
- **Sun 18 Oct:** Money manager 5–12 (forecast, payday view, cards, watchdog, low-balance warning, refunds, net worth) · Trading tracker (Hyperliquid by public address).
- **Mon 19 Oct:** Money manager 13–18 (side hustle and tax, shared costs, challenges, Ask/Talk, travel, year in review) · Reading and learning · Train-time queue completeness.
- **Tue 20 Oct:** Household and family sharing with his wife (needs Needs Meka #18) · Done for the day · Protected time · Edit your calendars completeness.
- **Wed 21 Oct:** "Hello, Navilife" greeting + orb + the companion (talk about anything) · Galaxy Watch completeness + approve drafts from the wrist · Vault · Travel mode completeness · Activity log and "What Navilife may do" completeness.
- **Thu 22 Oct:** Full news hub · Balance view · Clean-ups.
- **Fri 23 Oct:** Mac review polish (needs his Mac screenshots, #21) · completeness sweep across every item.
- **Sat 24 – Fri 31 Oct:** buffer: his feedback, the items waiting on keys (trains, travel times, spam lookup, DVLA, council bins, Enable Banking), polish. Target: everything done by **Fri 31 Oct**.

## Fix first (Meka's feedback, take before anything below)
- [~] **Take first (small, TOP; Meka 2026-10-29 11:21: "my orb keeps changing language: it says hello in English, then I ask a question and it replies in an Indian language").** Claude's hotfix 2026-10-29 (server only, no app update): Live's persona now says "RESPOND IN ENGLISH. YOU MUST RESPOND UNMISTAKABLY IN BRITISH ENGLISH in every turn…", the wording Google recommends for the native-audio models, which pick the reply language from what they hear (his question, in a noisy place or misheard, can come through as another language); and "if what you heard looks like another language or makes no sense, you probably misheard him: say so in English and ask again"; AiAskTest checks it. Build run 2026-10-29 11:30: (2) **Health → Obi's voice notes it** ("you were heard as another language" when Google's transcript of his words is mostly a non-Latin script, "Obi answered in another language" when Obi's is; once a turn, never his words; core LiveTalkLog/LiveTalkLogRules.otherScript, LiveTalkLogTest; Fold and Mac through the shared log); (3) **normal (non-Live) Obi** has the same English rule (AiAsk's prompt; AiAskTest). (1) `speechConfig.languageCode = "en-GB"` was **not added**: Google's forum reports the native-audio Live models refuse en-GB ("Unsupported language code") when the session opens, after the token is minted, so the server's retry wouldn't catch it and every Live talk would fall back to normal Obi; revisit only if Google adds en-GB for the model. What remains: the acceptance pass. *Check on the Fold (after the deploy and the update):* ask 5 questions on the orb with Live: every answer in English; if one isn't, Health → Obi's voice says whether you were heard as another language or Obi answered in one.
- [~] **Take first (TOP, ahead of the rest of Money; Meka 2026-10-28 19:32: "I asked Obi about my study and he said he had no view of that. Please ensure he sees everything I have").** Claude's check of the code: Ask's context (`AskMeka.kt`, the builder ending at `AskContext(...)`) carries tasks, events, shopping, school, meals, renewals, wins, queue, leave, facts, errands, appointments, checklists, goals, bins, car, gifts, people, trips and Trip lists, mail, kids, Barça and help, but **nothing from Study (courses, modules, lessons, the plan, planned sessions)**; and Live's tools are only `look_up_my_days` and `propose_action`, so on the orb Obi can't reach anything beyond the day's calendar and tasks. **The orb comes first** (Meka 19:45: "I tried via the orb. I need the orb to be the same, as I tend to go via that"): the orb is his main way in, so whatever typed Ask can see and offer, the orb must too, in Live and in the normal (non-Live) orb, with no gap. It should be like that for renewals, should it?").** Claude's check: a renewal made from an email (`MailRenewal.kt`, the fields written around "From a renewal email in your … mail") keeps only the account's name and the day it arrived, **not the message**, and the cost isn't carried over, so he has no way back to the email. Fix, for every item made from an email (renewals, bills, subscriptions, Life audit, and anything else `MailFindAuto` creates):
  (1) **Keep the source** (additive fields, no migration of old data): account id, message id, thread id, sender's name and address, subject, received day. Never the email's body.
  (2) **"Open the email"** on the card and its sheet: opens that exact message in Gmail (the Gmail app when installed, else the web link for that account) or Outlook; on the Mac, the browser. If the message is gone, say so.
  (3) **"From the email"** box on the card: sender, subject, received date, and what Navilife read from it (amount, plan, renew date, cancel-by), so he rarely needs to open it. Pre-fill **Cost** and **Cancel by** from the email when it states them.
  (4) **Old items** made before this (TeuxDeux, renewing Wed 25 Nov, from his Personal mail on Sun 25 Oct): find the message again by its merchant and day in that account, attach it, and fill the box; if none is found, show "Couldn't find the email again" instead of the button.
  (5) **The card follows the layout rules** (cards, numbers not sentences, no open inputs): today every chip row (Repeats, Show it, Cancel by, Kind) and two empty inputs are open on the card, and "Navilife never pays or cancels" is repeated on each one. Collapse to a summary card (name, renews date, cost, cancel-by, the From-the-email box, Open the email, Renewed · Cancelled it) with the rest behind "Edit" in a sheet, and the disclaimer once at the foot of the list. **Mockup first** for (5); (1)–(4) need none.
  Acceptance: tap TeuxDeux → Open the email → the renewal email opens; its card shows sender, subject, amount; a new renewal from email has cost filled. Both apps.

  **Layout rules for every Money tab (Meka 22:18: "it's sentences and text, not segmented, boxed, grouped, paginated… make it easy on the eye"), and from now on app-wide:** (a) every section is a **card** (rounded, #121214 on black, 16 dp padding, 12 dp between cards) with a small caps label on top; nothing floats as loose text on the page. (b) **Numbers, not sentences**: a figure is a tile (label 12 sp, value 17–20 sp semibold) or a row (name left, amount right); a review like "In August you spent £5,839.16 and £6,707.47 came in, so…" becomes 3 tiles + at most 2 short lines. - [~] **Take first (TOP, alongside Money; Meka 2026-10-26 20:27, angry: Live on the orb says "the same sentence 20 times and isn't working at all").** Claude pushed a hotfix (speaker = strict half-duplex while Obi speaks, `LiveEchoGate(bargeInOnSpeaker = false)`). But the tests show Google also stops Obi while only silence was going up, so the loop may not be echo at all. Find the real cause and prove it on the Fold: (a) set `realtimeInputConfig.activityHandling = NO_INTERRUPTION` in the Live setup when the output is the phone's speaker (and consider `automaticActivityDetection` with lower start sensitivity); (b) check whether a dropped socket + `sessionResumption` reconnect makes Gemini repeat its last turn, and whether the hello's words or the day's context are re-sent on each reconnect or each window; never replay a turn already spoken; (c) log per turn: interrupts (ours/Google's), reconnects, goAway, resumption, and the first 6 words of each spoken turn, and detect a repeat (same opening twice in 30 s) → stop, fall back to normal Obi with the reason on screen, never loop; (d) Health → Obi's voice shows the last talk's events. Acceptance: 5 questions on the speaker, each answered once, no repeats.
  - *What remains:* slice 1 (2026-10-26: **Live can no longer loop, and Health shows what each talk did**, both apps: Claude's read of the loop is the end of Obi's own sentence going back up as Meka's voice (the mic reopened 0.3 s after the clock said Obi finished, while the speaker's buffer and the room still carried him), Google answering it, and so on; now (a) on the speaker the session is locked to `activityHandling NO_INTERRUPTION` with low start-of-speech sensitivity (server, `Day.speaker`, additive; asked again without them if Google ever refuses), and the mic waits 0.8 s after Obi's voice (`LiveTalkRules.micHearsObi`); (b) a resumed window resends no hello (already so) and any replay is caught by (c): every turn's opening (first 6 words) is noted, and the same opening again within 30 s, unless he asked "say that again", stops Obi's voice at once and hands over to normal Obi with "Obi started repeating himself, so Live stopped" under the orb; the log notes Google's stops (voice or only silence going up), going away, fresh windows and carrying on; (d) Health → Obi's voice shows the last talk's last 8 events; core LiveTalkLog, LiveTalkLogTest, LiveConversationTest, LiveSoundFacadeTest, LiveSessionsTest; no migration; Mac checked nightly) landed. - [~] **Take first (TOP; Meka 2026-10-25 18:55, Live now connects: "starts off well with the intro, then I ask a question and it breaks when speaking and skips too").** Claude's read of `android/…/ask/LiveTalk.kt`: (a) **"skips"** = Google's `Interrupted` event, which clears the queue and flushes the track; on the speaker the mic keeps streaming whenever `LiveTalkRules.sendWhileSpeaking(output, db)` passes a fixed loudness, so Obi's own voice from the speaker (Samsung's echo canceller doesn't remove it all) reaches Google's voice detection, Google thinks Meka spoke, and cuts Obi off mid-sentence; (b) **"breaks"** = playback underruns: the AudioTrack is 0.2 s (`rate / 5 * 2` bytes) and starts playing the first chunk at once, so any network gap stutters. Fix: (1) **half-duplex on the speaker by default**: while Obi is talking out loud, send silence unless Meka's voice is clearly louder than Obi's own (mic level ≥ the played level + 12 dB for ≥ 250 ms, measured against what the track is playing right now, not a fixed threshold); full barge-in stays for headphones/Bluetooth; Health shows which mode. (2) **A jitter buffer**: track buffer ~1 s, start playback after 250–300 ms queued (or end of turn), and never flush on a gap. (3) Log every interrupt with mic dB, played dB, output route and whether it came from Google or us into the Speed log, and every underrun; the daily report counts them. (4) Test on the Fold's speaker at normal volume: 5 questions with long answers, no cut-offs, no stutters; then try talking over him on purpose: he stops within ~0.5 s. Unit tests for the gate (echo-level speech stays muted; his louder speech passes) and the jitter buffer's start rule.
  - *What remains:* slice 1 (2026-10-25: **Obi's echo is kept out and his voice no longer stutters**, both apps: on the speaker the microphone goes up while Obi talks only when Meka's voice is 12 dB over Obi's echo as the microphone hears it (learnt over the talk, never under -35 dBFS) for 250 ms, else as silence; headphones keep full barge-in; each turn's audio is held until 280 ms is queued (or the turn ends, or 450 ms passed), plays as one stream, waits again on a gap (counted as an underrun) instead of stuttering, and is dropped only when he talks over Obi; the Fold's track buffer is 1 s; core LiveEchoGate/LiveJitter, LiveAudioTest, LiveConversationTest; no server change, no migration; Mac checked nightly) landed. Slice 2 (2026-10-25: **Health shows how each Live talk sounded**: Obi's voice says speaker or headphones, how often Google stopped Obi for his voice with the mic and Obi levels at the last one (and whether only silence was going up), and how often and how long Obi waited for the network; a wait also shows in Health → Speed; both apps; core LiveAudioStats/LiveCutOffs, LiveSoundFacadeTest; no server change, no migration; Mac checked nightly) landed. Remaining: the daily report's count (waits on the optional "daily speed line" under Needs Meka); *Check on the Fold:* (4) 5 long answers on the speaker at normal volume with no cut-offs or stutters, then talk over him on purpose: he stops within about half a second.
- [~] **Take first (top, Meka 2026-10-25 01:45: "Everything takes a while and app feels slow. When I click on Done it takes at least 4 seconds, it should be instantaneous").** A rule for the whole app from now on: **every tap shows its result in the next frame (≤ 100 ms)**; the work runs after, and an error brings the card back with a line saying why. (1) **Done on a chat card** ("While you were at work" / Needs you, `NeedsYouRoute` onTriageChat): today the card only leaves when `core.seenTriageChat` returns, after `LiveReplies.markRead` and `WorkCaptureService.clear`; `seenTriageChat` resolves each message one by one on the single `onCore` thread (130 messages for "Tete 21 testing"), queued behind whatever else holds `onCore` (sync, mail, the face, speech). Fix: hide the card at once (a local "finishing" set the list filters on), resolve the chat's messages in **one** batched write/transaction, then mark read and clear; restore it if that fails. (2) **The same optimistic pattern for every quick move**: Done/Seen/Not now on every card, ticking a task or habit, moving a board card, Shared/category on a payment, Approve on mail clean-up, Look again. (3) **Find what holds `onCore`**: log any `onCore` block over 50 ms with its name to the Speed log, list the worst ten in Health → Speed and the daily report, and move long work (sync, mail sort, file fetches, AI calls) off the shared core thread or split it so taps never wait behind it. (4) **App-wide feel**: measure and report cold start to Today, tab switch, opening Money, Calendar and Ask; targets: cold start ≤ 1.5 s, tab switch ≤ 150 ms, any screen's first content ≤ 300 ms (cached first, refresh after). (4b) **A decided card must never come back** (Meka 2026-10-25 01:50: "clicked Approve and everything keeps coming back for me to approve again", the Clean up Personal 3,256 card): the swipe deck re-shows the card because the view still holds it until the queued `approveMailBacklog` → `refresh()` runs; keep a local "decided" set the deck and lists filter on until the view confirms (or the call fails, then the card returns with the reason). Also check, with a test, that after Approve the server's next `backlog()` write (`MailBacklogRules.fields`, which re-sends READY_AT) can't set `readyAtMs` later than `approvedAtMs` (that would hide the approval: `applying()` returns null when approvedAt < readyAt, and `canApprove` would offer it again); if it can, keep readyAt fixed once set.
(4c) **Money doesn't open** (Meka 2026-10-25 01:53: fingerprint accepted, the locked page stays): `openMoney()` is `onCore { …; refreshMoney() }`, so after the fingerprint it waits behind the queue and then builds the whole money view (2,900+ payments) before anything shows. Show the unlocked screen at once with the last cached view (or a skeleton), build the fresh view off the confined thread, and if the fingerprint result or the open fails, say so on the page instead of leaving it locked. Test: open returns to the screen in the next frame with a 3,000-payment fixture.
(5) **Likely root cause (Claude read the code 2026-10-25 01:50; Meka: "Got it" on Archived for you still nothing after 10 s):** every core call runs on the single `confined` thread (`onCore`), and almost every action ends with `refresh()` → `refreshOnce()`, ~330 lines that rebuild **every view in the app** (mail, money over 2,900 payments, family, board, archived, unopened, habits …), up to `MAX_REFRESH_PASSES` times; each sync arrival triggers it too. So one tap waits for one or more whole-app rebuilds queued ahead of it, and it gets slower as his data grows. Fix it properly: (a) **dirty flags**: each action and each synced record type marks only the areas it touches, and `refreshOnce` rebuilds only those (Got it → archived only; Done on a chat → triage only); (b) the heavy views (money, mail lists, board) are built off the confined thread from a snapshot and published when ready, never blocking a tap; (c) sync-triggered refreshes are coalesced (at most one every 500 ms, never while a tap is waiting); (d) time `refreshOnce` per area into the Speed log so Health shows "Money view 1.8 s" if one is slow. Test: a facade test with Meka-sized data (3,000 payments, 12,000 mail rows, 150 chat messages) where Got it, Done and a task tick each return in under 50 ms of core time.
Tests: a facade test that resolving a 130-message chat is one write; a Compose test that Done removes the card in the next frame while the core call is still suspended. Acceptance: on the Fold, Done clears instantly with 100+ messages in the chat, and Health → Speed shows no tap waiting over 100 ms.
  - *What remains:* slices 1–21 landed 2026-10-25/26 (history in the archive); slice 22 (2026-10-26: **a Compose test of the next-frame hide**: Done on Needs you's top card takes the card away once its fly-off ends while the core's write is still held behind a busy core thread, and once the core is free the write lands and the card never comes back; test-only, NeedsYouInstantTest) landed. Remaining: (2) any other quick move Health → Speed shows as slow; (3, rest) the worst ten and the feel times in the daily report (waits on the optional "daily speed line" under Needs Meka); (4, rest) Calendar's and Ask's own first content if Speed shows them slow; (5) keep-if-unchanged / off the core thread for anything else Speed shows as slow; *Check on the Fold:* open the app cold, switch tabs, open Money, then Health → Speed lists the three with their aims.

Fix: (1) key every cached clip by the exact voice (engine + voice id + rate) and drop all cached clips whose voice isn't the current one, on every device, at next open and whenever the voice changes; (2) one voice resolver used by the stage, Ask, Talk, the brief, the bedside clock, speaking-up, calls and the Mac, so they can never differ; (3) remake the openers in Obi's voice in the background; (4) test: change voice → no clip from the old voice is ever played; the stage and Ask ask for the same voice id. Health → Obi's voice names the voice in use ("Brian (Polly)" / the Chirp voice) so Meka can see it.
  - *What remains:* slice 1 (2026-10-24: **old clips in another voice are never played**: cause: the phone's clip memory is keyed by the voice *asked for*, and "the server's default" is one key whatever the default is, so hello openers made while the default was a woman's voice kept playing after the server switched to Brian (the app stays in memory across a server deploy), while Ask's new words came fresh in Brian. Now each clip remembers the voice that actually spoke it; when a newer clip for the same choice comes back in another voice, the older ones are dropped and remade (core SpeechRules.stale), and every 15-minute hello warm first checks the server's default voice so they're remade before the next tap; Health → Obi's voice shows "Speaking as Brian" on the Fold and the Mac; no server change, no migration; Mac checked nightly) landed; b7eb5f6, CI run 1215 (40129674187) green. AQ. key? falling back silently to normal Talk): Health and the stage must say "Live" vs "Normal (Live unavailable: …reason)" every turn. The cut-off speech is most likely **barge-in hearing Obi's own voice through the phone's speaker** (the mic stays open during and after he speaks, conversation mode; echo is only filtered with headphones), or the next clip failing: use Android's acoustic echo canceller / VOICE_COMMUNICATION audio source on the speaker, require real speech (his voice, ≥ 2 words, not Obi's own words) before stopping, and log why a clip stopped (barged in / clip failed / replaced). Original item: Targets were ≤ 1.5 s to his voice. Read the device Speed log and the server's own timings (both exist) for today's turns and name where the 10 s goes (endpointing wait, recogniser final, AI first token, Polly/Chirp clip, the hello cache miss, the stage fix's re-composition of Ask's field, a retry or timeout) in the daily report; fix the biggest two with tests; make Speed visible from Health → Obi's voice ("last answer 9.8 s: 6.1 s waiting for the AI"). Meka's key "Navilife Live" is bound to the service account ais-gemini-key-…, which is the right kind; he was told not to make another. So the fault is ours or a permission: (a) the server calls Google with the stored key at start-up and on each Live request, sending it as the `x-goog-api-key` header (not `?key=`), and records Google's exact status and message; (b) Health → Obi's voice shows it ("Live ready" / "Google said 401 …" / "no key"); (c) the client never quietly falls back to normal Talk without a visible reason; (d) if it's a 401/403 on a bound key, Needs Meka gets the exact console step (e.g. the IAM role the service account needs, or Gemini API added to the key's API restrictions), with the error text quoted. Tests: an AQ.-shaped key passes the parser (`GoogleMapsKey.parse` already accepts 20–200 chars), the header is used, and the error reaches Health.

(5) **A weekly whole-app walk-through** (Sunday night build run): screenshot every screen on both Fold sizes and the Mac, review them with fresh eyes against `docs/design/` and the checklist (alignment, spacing rhythm, empty states, broken numbers, touch targets ≥ 48 dp, animation present where Meka likes it), and **file what's wrong as Take first items before Meka finds them**, listed in the Monday report as "Found and fixing". (6) The first walk-through runs now, before the next feature: every screen, findings filed. A separate reviewer (fresh agent, not the one that built it) does (2) and (5).

- [~] **Take first (Meka 2026-10-20 19:57, do together with the hello item below): the orb feels slow, "a lot of wait".** Measure first, then cut every wait. (1) **A timing trace** on each Talk/Hello turn (device-only, no words): tap → first sound, end of his speech → request sent, → first answer byte, → first audio, clip fetch times; the last 20 turns shown in Ask → Settings → Navilife's voice → "Speed" so we see where the time goes, and a one-line summary in the daily build report. (2) **Targets**: tap → hello audible ≤ 0.6 s; he stops talking → Navilife starts answering ≤ 1.5 s (quick look-ups ≤ 1 s); a spoken answer never pauses between sentences. (3) **Cuts**: the hello starts **during** the entrance, not after it (today the greeting waits ~1.2 s for scatter + gather), and the entrance can be tapped through; the hello openers' audio is **made ahead and cached** on the device (refreshed in the background, so no voice fetch on tap); the next sentence's clip is fetched while the current one plays; listening ends 0.6 s after he stops (now 0.8 s) with the recogniser's partial results sent early for quick look-ups; the server keeps its AI and voice connections warm, picks the small model for the first sentence of an open answer when that is faster; Talk opens the mic while the hello is still speaking so he can just start talking (barge-in already stops it); a gentle "thinking" sound/orb motion appears at once so silence never feels dead. (4) If the network is slow, say a short holding line ("One sec, Meka…") from the cache after 1.2 s. Fold, bedside clock and Mac. Tests: entrance greet time ≤ 300 ms; cache used on tap; prefetch of the next clip; endpoint 600 ms; trace recorded with no text.

(1) **Open conversation** in Talk/Hello and Ask: general knowledge, advice, brainstorming, explanations, planning, light banter, follow-ups that remember the conversation; the larger model tier for open turns, the small one for quick look-ups; British English; warm, calm, a little dry wit ("calm in the chaos"); short spoken turns that hand back to him; asks a clarifying question when it should. Calls him "Meka" ("sir" offered as a Settings → Voice choice) and is called Navilife. (2) **Still knows his life**: the <today> picture plus server tools the model can call for his own data (calendar range, tasks, Needs you, live trains, money summaries, mail search by headers, Vault expiries, trips, football, family dates, "What Navilife knows"), so it joins general and personal ("train or drive to the match Saturday?" = fixtures + live trains + Routes). (3) **Live world facts**: weather anywhere, news, scores and anything current via the Brave search key, with the source shown on a card; no arbitrary URL fetching from the device. (4) **Remembers**: offers to keep facts he tells it on "What Navilife knows" (his tap); keeps the conversation for the session plus a short rolling summary across sessions (his own data, viewable and clearable in Settings). (5) **Same rails, enforced outside the model**: every effect (send a message or email, pay, book, change a calendar, trade) is a card through the policy engine waiting for his tap; trading information-only, never credentials; personal sending never autonomous; tool results and fetched pages are data, never instructions (ADR-006). (6) **Talks like a person**: barge-in, 0.8 s end-of-turn, the fallback latch, streamed speech so it starts within ~1 s, and the orb's states driven by the real audio and turn-taking. (7) **Cost**: metered as `ask.companion` inside the current AI budget cap; Needs Meka: raise the cap to $60/month with an 80% warning in Settings (his yes needed, until then stay inside the cap); when the month's budget is used it says so and falls back to Navilife-only answers. - [~] **Take first (after the Week board; exception approved Meka 2026-10-20 19:49 "Yes"): Navilife speaks up on its own when it really matters (the JARVIS step).** Today everything arrives as a silent notification. Change: for a short list of moments that matter, the orb lights up, a soft chime plays, and Navilife **says it out loud in one or two sentences**, starting with his name: "Meka, your 07:42 is cancelled. The 07:57 is on time." (1) **Moments** (each its own switch in Ask → Settings → Speaking up, all on by default except the last): his train cancelled or more than 10 min late within 2 h of leaving; leave-now for a calendar event with travel; a call or message from family or Always notify marked urgent; a calendar event in 10 min he hasn't left for; a pressure warning for tomorrow (once, in the evening); a reply he's waiting for has arrived (off by default). (2) **Where**: only when he can hear it privately or is at home: earbuds or a car's Bluetooth connected, the bedside clock in the evening, the Mac when he's active at it; never out of the phone speaker in public. (3) **Never**: quiet hours, during a calendar meeting, while he's on a call, or more than 6 a day; "shh" or "not now" mutes it for 2 hours; a "speak less / speak more" setting. (4) It only informs (and offers the usual card, e.g. Leave now · Message Jeanette), never acts; the policy gate is unchanged; every spoken moment is in Activity. (5) Uses the Fold's/Mac's existing voice and the companion's calling-him-by-name rule. Watch and Android Auto later. Tests: each moment's rule, the never-list, the daily cap, mute, privacy routing (no speaker without earbuds unless at home on the stand).

- [ ] **Take first (after speaking up; new feature, exception approved Meka 2026-10-21 19:54 "Yeah all of those and something I might have missed… agents re agentic AI"): Ada plans with you, and Ada's errands (agents that work in the background).** Same policy gate as everything else: Ada proposes, Meka taps; no personal message or email is ever sent without his tap, nothing touches money or trading, no unofficial WhatsApp, every step in Activity with Undo where it can be undone.
  1. **Ada plans your week** — "Ada, plan my week" (or the Sunday 18:00 Plan your week) has Ada draft the whole week on the Board: tasks spread by due dates, estimates, free time, goals, office days, the kids' fixtures and how he said he's feeling; shown as ghost cards with "Keep all · Change · Start over", and talked through out loud if he asked by voice ("I've put the council tax on Thursday morning and kept Friday light"). "Ada, plan my day" the same for today, re-plannable at any time ("I lost the afternoon, re-plan").
  2. **Ada's suggestions on the Board** (Sunsama-like, quietly): a small gold ✦ on a card when Ada has a thought: "This has slipped 3 times, break it into 2?", "Usually takes you 45m, not 15m", "Thursday's over by 1h, move this to Saturday?", "This email needs a reply, add 15m?"; one tap applies, a swipe dismisses; never more than 3 on screen.
  3. **Ada's errands (the agents)**: one place, Ask → **Ada is working on**, listing jobs she's running over hours or days, each with its steps, what she's found, and the one decision she needs from him. He starts one by asking ("Ada, find someone to service the boiler", "plan half term", "chase the council about the bin", "compare car insurance before renewal", "get me three quotes for the fence"). Ada then works step by step on her own: researching on the web (Brave), reading his own mail and calendar for context, drafting the emails or messages needed, putting holds in the calendar, adding tasks to the Board, and checking back for replies; **every outgoing message or booking waits as a card for his tap**. Standing errands she runs without being asked, each switchable: **Renewals watcher** (MOT, insurance, TV licence, subscriptions found in mail: reminds 4 weeks ahead and offers to compare), **Birthday keeper** (family birthdays: a heads-up 10 days ahead with gift ideas and a draft message, so he never forgets), **Inbox keeper** (today's mail autopilot, reported here), **Follow-up chaser** (things he's waiting on: drafts a nudge after N days), **School & clubs** (term dates, forms, kit, fixtures into the week). No dashboard of status lights: a simple list, newest first, each a card with "What I did · What I need from you".
  - Runs on the server on a schedule with the larger model for planning, small for routine checks; metered per errand inside the AI cap (Meka 2026-10-21 19:59: yes to $60/month; MEKA_AI_BUDGET_USD=60 set in the stack); each errand has a daily step limit and stops and asks when unsure. Fold, Mac, and Ada can be asked aloud "what are you working on?". Tests: no send without a tap (policy), step caps, errands resume after a restart, each standing errand's trigger, ghost-card plan applies/undoes as one.

But it should be intuitive enough to just add this rather than asking for another confirmation. Also most times it doesn't understand me… when I use Todoist's AI mic it understands everything I say").** Found in the code: Talk sends his words to the AI, waits, speaks the answer, then shows an `add_task` card that waits for his yes (ADD_TASK only offers to go automatic after five yeses in a row); the capture sheet's 🎙 opens the system recogniser dialog (`EXTRA_PREFER_OFFLINE`, no language set) and then still waits for ↑ Add; and every listener is on-device only (ADR-006: `createOnDeviceSpeechRecognizer` on the Fold, `requiresOnDeviceRecognition` on the Mac, the watch's own), which mishears him. Build, in this order:
  1. **Just add it.** His own words asking to add a task or a shopping item ("add …", "remind me to …", "put … on my list / shopping", "I need to …") are done at once with the undo chip, never a card: a no-AI `TalkReply` like ReadOut and Open (title cleaned of the command words; "today", "tomorrow", "Friday" read as the due day), spoken back short ("Added: call the garage, tomorrow."), shown as "Added “…” · Undo"; it works when the AI is unreachable too, and the same through Ask's typed field. ADD_TASK and ADD_SHOPPING count as trusted for his own direct requests (What Navilife may do keeps **Ask first** to switch back; other people's words still never add anything, rule 1).   2. **The capture sheet's mic adds without the extra tap.** After he speaks, the words go straight in as a task with "Added “…” · Undo · Edit" for a few seconds (Edit reopens the field with the words); typing still uses ↑ Add. The watch already works this way.
  3. **Fast.** Partial words on screen while he speaks; listening ends about 0.8 s after he stops (not the recogniser's long default); measure end of speech → "Added" on the phone (an Activity/Health line) and keep it under about a second.
  4. **Understand him.** *Meka said yes (2026-10-17 11:40, in chat: "use Google's online speech when I have signal"):* Google's online recogniser whenever there's signal (Fold: `SpeechRecognizer.createSpeechRecognizer` without PREFER_OFFLINE, en-GB, partial results; Mac: `requiresOnDeviceRecognition = false`; watch the same), the on-device one only when offline; ADR-006 addendum (his yes is the third-party sharing approval rule 5 asks for).

(1) **Server**: a second `SpeechEngine`, `GoogleSpeech`, calling Cloud Text-to-Speech `v1/text:synthesize` with an API key (MP3, en-GB) for **Chirp 3: HD** voices (list them from `v1/voices?languageCode=en-GB`, keep the `Chirp3-HD` ones; also en-US as a second group); the key is read from Secrets Manager `meka-os-dev/speech/google` (`{"api_key":"…"}`), falling back to `meka-os-dev/maps/google` if absent (the same key can be allowed for both APIs); empty/absent = Google voices hidden and Polly stays as today. Server-side only; never on a device. (2) **Engine choice and fallback**: the chosen voice carries its provider (google/polly); on a Google failure or timeout the same sentence is retried once on the Polly default (never the phone's voice unless both fail, per the existing wait rules), and Health notes "Google voice failed · used Polly". (3) **Cost**: Chirp 3 HD is free for the first 1M characters a month, then $30/1M; meter characters per provider in the existing month usage, show "Google voice · 84k of 1M free this month" in Settings → Voice, and fall back to Polly automatically past 900k (never paid without his yes). (4) **Voice sampler** (Ask → Settings → Voice, both apps): the voices grouped by provider (Google Chirp 3 HD British first, then Polly), each row with a ▶ that plays the same sample — his real morning brief's first two sentences (or a stock line if none yet) — cached per voice so replays are free; tap a row to choose ("Navilife will speak with Aoede"), with a brief waveform/pulse animation while it plays; a separate choice per use is optional later (one voice for now). The brief, Talk, the bedside clock and the call assistant's greeting (Twilio <Play> of a cached MP3) all use the chosen voice. (5) **Natural delivery**: send the brief as sentences with SSML-free natural punctuation (Chirp 3 HD doesn't take SSML); keep the script rules (numbers and times written as said, e.g. "ten past seven"). (6) Needs Meka #33 (enable the Text-to-Speech API on the same Google Cloud project and allow it on the key). Until then the sampler shows the Polly voices and a line "More natural Google voices · add the key" with the steps. Tests: provider choice, fallback to Polly, free-tier cap, sampler cache; a recorded sample response, no real key.

- [~] **Take first: spam calls still ring (Meka, 2026-10-13 20:51: a missed call from an unknown number that the phone's own caller ID labelled as an official organisation rang through; "filtering of spam calls should be on all the time, and when it identifies one it should be added to blocked so such calls don't even ring").** Spam protection exists (MekaCallScreeningService: the synced block list is rejected silently; with the switch on, failed caller checks / withheld in quiet hours go to the assistant; "Unknown caller · Block?" afterwards) but this call rang. **Checked 20:54: Navilife IS the Caller ID and spam app (role held; the launcher shows the Navilife N3·2 icon, so the new build is in).** So the call reached the screening service and the rules let it through (not on the list, not a contact, and the network check didn't fail or wasn't on) — the fix is the rules in (3), not the role. (1) **Diagnose first**: report to the deploy run's annotations (like the notification trace) whether Navilife holds the call-screening role (`RoleManager.ROLE_CALL_SCREENING`), the spam switch's state, and the last 20 screened calls' verdicts with reasons (number hashed, never the digits) — so we know whether this call reached the service at all. (2) **Always on**: spam protection is on by default and has no off switch in normal use (only inside Work mode → Calls → Advanced, with a warning); if the role is lost (Samsung's own caller ID app taking it back, an update), Today shows a red "Spam calls can ring · Fix" line that requests the role, and Setup/Health show it red. (3) **Auto-block, no prompt**: a number is added to the block list automatically (with Undo in Activity and a quiet line "Blocked 07… · it won't ring again · Undo") when: the network's caller check failed; or the call assistant screened it and the caller hung up or gave no name/reason; or the same unknown number called 2+ times in 7 days without leaving a voicemail; or he swiped Block on the after-call notice. Never auto-block: contacts, anyone he called in 90 days, numbers in the household profile, withheld/private (those go to the assistant, not the list), and **numbers the phone identifies as an emergency service, police, NHS, school or bank** — those ring through but get a heads-up after: "Calls claiming to be police/bank can be spoofed · call back on 101 / the number on your card, not this one". (4) Blocked calls never ring or notify; they stay in the call log and in Activity → Blocked calls, with Unblock. The block list stays in his encrypted, synced data (rule 13: no numbers in the repo — move the seeded 9 Oct number out of code into his data in the same slice). (5) Android doesn't let a non-dialer app write the system "Blocked numbers" list, so Navilife's list is enforced by its own screening (same result: no ring); Settings explains this in one line. Tests: auto-block rules (each trigger, each never-block), role-lost line.

  - *Look it up and let the assistant talk to them (Meka, 2026-10-13 20:56: "can the app look the number up on the internet, and if people have reported it, pick it up, talk to them, and if it's fake drop and block"):* (a) **Number reputation, fast**: for a caller who isn't a contact/recent/household, the screening service asks the server (2.5 s budget; Android gives ~5 s) for the number's reputation: cached verdicts first, else a web search for the number (a search API — Needs Meka: a free-tier key, e.g. Brave Search API, in Secrets Manager) whose snippets from report sites (who-called/tellows-style pages) the AI reads into {scam · nuisance/sales · legit business · unknown} with a confidence and one-line reason; cached 30 days per number (hashed); nothing about the number in logs. (b) **Act on it**: reported scam with high confidence → reject before it rings and auto-block (Activity: "Blocked 0161… · reported 23× as a police-impersonation scam · Undo"); nuisance/sales → straight to the assistant; unknown or the lookup timed out → the assistant (default) or ring, his choice in Calls ("Unknown callers: Assistant first / Ring"). (c) **The assistant talks to them** (live Twilio number, carrier forward-when-busy): asks who's calling and why; the AI weighs their answer with the lookup (claims of police/bank/HMRC/courier with payment or 'verify your details' = scam signals); genuine and urgent → it offers to put them through: it calls his mobile from the assistant's number (always allowed through) with a whisper "Dentist about Thursday · press 1 to take it, 2 for a message"; genuine not urgent → takes a message (summary in Needs you); judged fake → a polite close ("Meka will contact the organisation directly"), hang up, auto-block, and a heads-up with the transcript summary and Undo. (d) **Never** let the assistant share any of his details, confirm his name/address, or agree to anything; it announces it's an automated assistant; anyone claiming police/bank is never put through on their word — he gets "Claimed to be police · call 101 to check". Emergency numbers and the household are never screened. (e) Cost shown in Calls (lookups are cached; assistant minutes a few pence) and counted in the AI budget; tests with invented numbers and transcripts.
- [~] **Phone updates sometimes fail to install (Meka, 2026-10-10 16:37 and 2026-10-12 06:15; take first).** `AppUpdater` shows only a generic line and drops Android's own reason. Fix on the Fold: (1) keep `PackageInstaller.EXTRA_STATUS_MESSAGE` and the status code, show them under the failure line and in Activity; (2) report the failure to MEKA's server (status code, Android's message, build numbers, free-storage band, network type — nothing personal) via a signed POST so a build run can read why; Health shows the last failure; (3) a broken download retries itself up to 3 times with backoff (Range resume where supported) before failing; (4) STATUS_FAILURE_ABORTED after a dismissed prompt returns to Ready without an error; (5) if the phone was locked when the prompt came, show it on next open instead of failing; (6) Try again reuses a cached download whose hash matches. Tests for the failure text and retry rules; then read the reported reason and fix its root cause in the same item.
  - *What remains:* slice 1 (2026-10-12, d7a7dd5 + c99588b, a server compile fix; CI run 425 (38209575883) green) built (1)–(6) on the Fold and the server; details in the archive. Left: **read the reported reason and fix its root cause** — after Meka's next failed update the Fold reports it; the deploy workflow's `publish-fold` job prints the last 14 days' reports as `::warning title=Phone update failed::…` annotations (read with the check-runs annotations API on that job). Until a report arrives, nothing buildable here: take the next item. One likely cause already fixed: Install ran in the Today card's screen scope, so leaving Today mid-download cancelled it.
  - *Check on the Fold:* tap Install on an update and switch to another tab while it downloads: it carries on and Android's prompt comes. Dismiss the prompt: the card goes back to Install with no error. If an install fails, the card says why with Android's own words under it, Activity lists "Build … didn't install", Ask → More → Health → Updates shows it with Try again (which reuses the downloaded file).
  - *Found 2026-10-13 11:12 (deploy run 525's `publish-fold`):* build 942 (slice 2, cd8b4be) → 973 still failed **"Session files in use"** once (then went in on a second go at 11:12). **Slice 3 (2026-10-13) fixes the root cause** (core + Fold, Mac checked nightly; 9d61985, CI run 526 (38397465777) green first try; details in the archive): Android checks every file written into a session has been let go at commit, and the write's close reaches it asynchronously, so committing straight after closing raced it. Each commit now waits 1 s after the write (2.5/5/8 s on each fresh session), up to 3 fresh sessions are tried by themselves, and if Android still says "in use" the kept download goes to **Android's own installer** (a FileProvider URI, same one-tap prompt) instead of failing; the report says "· N sessions tried" (and "· handed to Android's installer"). What remains: Meka confirming the next updates install; read `publish-fold`'s "Phone update failed" annotations after updates: "handed to Android's installer" or "4 sessions tried" there means the race is still being hit.
  - *Slice 4 (2026-10-19): **late answers dropped, and the fallback is left alone*** — deploy 918 showed "Session files in use" on all four sessions (17 and 19 Oct), then a late "Session destroyed" and "Failed to override installation location" after the hand-over. Answers from abandoned sessions are now dropped instead of failing the card, and a build already handed to Android's installer goes straight back there on the next tap; core + Fold, no migration, Mac checked nightly; eba75eb. **Fix (2026-10-19):** eba75eb also had the session read the kept file itself on Android 14+ (`Session.write` with a file descriptor), but that is a hidden system API: CI run 915's Android compile failed ("Unresolved reference 'write'"). The follow-up drops it: every Android version writes through `openWrite` again (closed before commit, with the settle wait), and reports no longer say "written from the file"; beb3edc, CI run 916 (39285759348) green. Left: read the next "Phone update failed" annotations. If "Session files in use" still shows on every fresh session, the next idea is to copy the download into the session from a fresh file each time, or to rely on the hand-over to Android's installer, which already works.

Forget all clears it; Don't remember starts fresh each time. The Mac's voice sheet the same.

If Android's own installer screen ("Do you want to install this update?") comes instead, tap Install there; tapping Install again on the card brings that same screen back rather than an error.

- [~] **The commute counts everywhere (Meka, 2026-10-12 20:51, shutdown's wake alarm: "Suggested from Work at 09:00 · 1 h to get ready" → 08:00 — it doesn't factor in the train journey).** Take next (the icon swap landed 2026-10-13). (1) A **door-to-door commute time** per direction in Work mode (home → office, office → home), default 1 h 20 (train + change + walks) and editable; on office days only (WFH/off days: none). (2) **Wake alarm** on an office day = work start − morning commute − time to get ready, e.g. 09:00 − 1 h 20 − 1 h = 06:40, with the line "Work at 09:00 · 1 h 20 commute · 1 h to get ready"; when live departures exist, anchor on the train: "Leave by 07:30 for the 07:43 · wake 06:30". (3) **Today's timeline and the Calendar** show the commute as a thin band before and after the office block ("Train · 07:40–09:00", "Home ~18:50"), not as events. (4) **Clashes use arrival home, not work end**: an event before home-arrival on an office day is flagged ("Training 18:00 — you're home about 18:50 · who's taking?"), in Today, the Calendar, the brief and the week ahead. - [~] Voice switches back to robotic after one answer (Meka, 2026-10-09 12:43). Cause: Talk waited only 1.2 s (`SpeechRules.FIRST_AUDIO_MS`) for MEKA's voice; on mobile data the phone → CloudFront → server → Polly round trip was often slower, and a miss hands the **whole answer** to the phone's voice. Fixed interactively: 4 s first-piece wait (tests updated; CI green 2026-10-09 in run 37925994507, which carried 30602b5 with the messages assistant's slice 1). Next run also: (1) measure — log each clip's time-to-audio on the Fold and show the last 10 in Activity ("MEKA's voice · 1.8 s"); (2) cut latency: request the first sentence's audio the moment the AI's first sentence arrives (stream the AI reply sentence by sentence), keep the server's Polly client warm, return audio as binary not base64 JSON; (3) never switch voice mid-conversation: if a later answer misses, say "One moment…" in the cached MEKA voice and keep waiting up to 6 s before falling back.
  - *What remains:* completeness slice 1 (2026-10-19: **offline is said, and Bluetooth is counted** — when Navilife's voice can't be reached the phone's voice starts with "Using the phone's voice while offline." once (the Mac's own words), not again until Navilife's voice is heard; Activity's timing line marks clips heard over Bluetooth or car audio "+ Bluetooth" and counts about 0.2 s for it; the device-voice choice, cancelling pending clips on Stop and the Mac's same waits were already there; core + Fold + Mac, help tip, nothing stored, no migration, Mac checked nightly; bd4c0c9 + 5baf90f (the offline mark kept on the core after the request, not inside its timeout), CI run 904 (39237613293) green; run 903 also failed once on AskMekaFacadeTest's train-lines test, which passed on 904 as on 744; details in the archive) landed. Completeness slice 2 (2026-10-19: **the voice in the AI budget** — every Polly clip (and the start-up warm line) is counted in the month's AI budget as Voice at Polly's list price (generative $30, neural $16 a million characters), shown in Settings → Navilife's AI as "Voice · 301 clips · 60¢" and in Activity's day line as "Voice 12"; once the budget is spent the server answers over and the device's own voice speaks until the 1st; Google's clips stay inside its free tier and aren't counted; server SpeechService(budget) + PollyPrices + AiBudget.recordCost/over, core AiTodayRules.Group.VOICE, both apps through the shared lines, help tip, ADR-006 addendum, no migration, Mac checked nightly; c09d7ed, CI run 905 (39245590362) green first try; details in the archive) landed. Slice 3 (2026-10-19: **the first words come with the answer** — a spoken question asks the server to say the answer's first piece in the chosen voice while it still holds the answer (within 2.5 s, through the same meters and budget), and the device plays it from memory without a second round trip; Activity marks it "with the answer"; never typed questions or the device's own voice; wire + server + core + facade, no app change, help tip, ADR-006 addendum, nothing stored, no migration, Mac checked nightly; d0e8906, CI run 906 (39248390899) green first try; details in the archive) landed; streaming the reply itself isn't needed for one-or-two-sentence answers. Evening is the reverse (work (London) → Farringdon → home; fallback via King's Cross). Morning window default: trains arriving Farringdon by ~08:40 (learn and adjust from the train he actually takes; editable).

Make both wait (idle the looper, advance the clock) until the text shows, up to a bound, before asserting; never skip them. *Also (run 1301, 2026-10-26 17:49):* `WorkScreensTest.byHand` ("Start work now" not on screen after Stop work now; its wait loops use `return@repeat`, which never stops early): the same fix.

## Milestones

### M0 · Foundation — done

### M1 · Run my day
- [~] Call assistant for mobile calls at work (Needs Meka #9; code done, waiting on Needs Meka #13): during work mode, MEKA's call screening lets family, the always-ring list and repeat callers (2nd call within 3 min) ring; other calls are declined, and the carrier's "forward when busy" sends them to a cloud phone number where a voice assistant says it is Meka's assistant, that Meka is at work and will call back, takes a message, and asks if it's urgent. Messages land in the after-work summary; urgent ones alert Meka immediately. Fixed greeting written by Meka; the assistant always says it is an automated assistant; one switch turns it off. **Approved 2026-10-07** (paid phone service OK; greeting = "Hi, you've reached Meka's assistant. Meka is at work right now and will call you back. Can I take a message, and is it urgent?"). Provider: Twilio (UK number + voice). Build and test everything behind a provider interface first (call screening on the Fold, webhook + voice flow on the server, messages into the after-work summary, urgent → Critical alert); then add Needs Meka #13 for Meka to open the Twilio account, buy the UK number and set "forward when busy".
  - *Remaining:* Needs Meka #13 (the Twilio account, number and forwarding), then a live test call. *Check on the Fold:* whether a declined call posts Samsung's missed-call notification (the summary relies on it); if not, the screening service should hold the call itself.
  - *Low-balance guard (Meka, 2026-10-08, auto-recharge OFF by choice):* **built 2026-10-10 (below).** The server reads the Twilio balance daily (`GET /2010-04-01/Accounts/{sid}/Balance.json`, same secret) and MEKA shows a Needs you card "Call assistant credit low · £4.20 left · Top up" (opens twilio.com/console/billing) below £5, and a heads-up below £2. If the account is suspended or the balance is unreadable, the Fold stops declining calls during work (they ring as normal) so callers are never sent to a dead number; Work screen says "Call assistant paused · Twilio credit".
  - *Low-balance guard, 2026-10-10 (server + core + Fold + Mac; core and macos touched, so Mac checked nightly):* **Core** (CallCredit.kt, non-AI, pure; `CallCreditTest`, 9): a server-authored `context_mode/call_credit` entity (ADR-008 addendum: `state` OK · LOW under £5 · VERY_LOW under £2 · PAUSED, `pence`, `currency`, `since`, `reason` EMPTY under 50p · SUSPENDED · UNREADABLE after a day of failed reads) read by `CallCreditStore`; `CallCreditRules.assess` (what the server writes after a check; a failed read keeps what the devices have for 24 h), `card` (Needs you: "Call assistant credit low" · "£4.20 left · top up so callers can leave a message", or "Call assistant paused · Twilio credit" · "Out of credit · £0.12 left · calls ring as usual at work until it's sorted"; Top up / Open Twilio open twilio.com/console/billing, a new `DecisionEffect.OPEN_LINK` and `DecisionKind.CREDIT`; lit and first in the stack under £2 or paused, else after the tasks; only while the assistant is switched on), `notices` (a new governor source **Call assistant credit**, heads-up once when it drops under £2 and once when it pauses), `healthLine` (Health's call assistant row: "On · £4.20 left · top up soon" / "Paused · …"). `CallScreeningRules.decide(paused)`: while paused every call that would have gone to the assistant rings (`CallReason.PAUSED`; the block list still blocks), and `statusLine(paused)` says "Paused · Twilio credit · calls ring as usual until it's topped up"; `WorkModeState.callAssistantPaused`. **Server** (backend/CallCredit.kt): `CreditSource` on `TwilioVoice` (the account's status and `Balance.json`, same secret, nothing new), `CallCreditWatch` writes only the fields that changed, over each field's head, and wakes the devices; `startCreditWatch` 2 minutes after start, then every 6 hours (hourly while reads fail); nothing is read until the account SID and token are in the secret. **Fold:** Needs you's Top up opens the browser; the Work screen's line. **Mac:** the same in `CoreModel.decide` (`.openLink`) and the Work sheet's line; bridge `testCallCreditReachesSwift`. Tests: `CallCreditTest` (core), `CallCreditFacadeTest` (what the server writes reaches screening and Needs you), backend `CallCreditWatchTest` (4: only changes written, a day of failed reads pauses, nothing before setup, Twilio's two reads). Motion: the Call assistant row's line and the card on the Needs you stack (catalogue updated). Once it is under, Needs you shows "Call assistant credit low · £… left" with Top up (opens Twilio's billing page); Health → Call assistant says the amount. If it ever runs out, the Work screen says "Paused · Twilio credit" and work calls ring as usual until you top up (within 6 hours of topping up it switches back on its own).

- [~] **Money manager (Meka approved 2026-10-12 21:19: Open Banking via Enable Banking, read-only).** **Where it lives (Meka, 2026-10-13 18:05): a Money tile in Ask's Life grid, not a new bottom tab** — it opens the Money screen (Overview · Cards · Subscriptions · Net worth · Trading) behind biometric; day-to-day it surfaces in Today/the brief (safe to spend, bills, card reminders), Needs you (watchdog alerts) and Ask/Talk. Data: Enable Banking "restricted production" (free for accounts he links himself) — consent on his bank's own screen, read-only (accounts, balances, transactions), re-consent when the bank asks (~90 days, a Setup/Today line a week before), revoke any time; **never** payment initiation, never moves money, never stores bank passwords. Security for a public repo: the Enable Banking application id + RSA private key live only in Secrets Manager (`meka-os-dev/money/enablebanking`), consent/session ids and all transactions only in his encrypted database (KMS) and synced store; nothing financial in logs, CI, fixtures or commits (tests use invented transactions); the server calls Enable Banking with a JWT signed by that key; ADR addendum. Setup steps for Meka under Needs Meka when slice 1 lands (create the app in Enable Banking's control panel, redirect URL https://d2o7ogpu114nmw.cloudfront.net/v1/money/callback, paste the key into the secret). Features (slices): (1) Connect bank (per bank, his choice) + accounts and balances; (2) transactions synced daily, **categorised** (merchant rules + MCC, AI only for unclear ones, words only; corrections teach), receipts from email matched for item detail; (3) **Money screen**: this week/month spend by category vs earnings, trend vs his 6-month average, subscriptions list + monthly total + renewal dates with Cancel help; (4) **specific nudges from his own numbers** ("Coffee 19× · £71 this month · halving saves ~£430/yr", "Clothes £180 over your 6-month average"), never generic tips; (5) **forecast**: at current trend balance/savings in 6 and 12 months, with what-ifs (cut a category by X, extra income from the side hustle or trading at £/month — trading figures only from Kestrel's existing event channel, no credentials); (6) payday view "safe to spend until payday", bills due this week, money goals linked to Goals; (7) monthly money review in plain English (AI, facts only), side-hustle P&L from the business account and its bank account if linked. Not regulated advice: insight on his own spending, no investment recommendations. **Also (Meka asked 2026-10-12 21:22 to add what he missed; all read-only, all insight not advice):** (8) **Cards**: each card's statement balance, due date and minimum, a reminder 3 days before ("pay in full to avoid interest"; the charge card always in full), utilisation %, and 0%/promo periods ending; interest or late fees charged get flagged. (9) **Watchdog alerts**: unusual or large transactions, possible duplicates, foreign transaction fees, a free trial about to become paid, a subscription or bill price rise ("up £2 from next month"), money leaving that he doesn't recognise → "Is this you?" with a link to the bank's app (MEKA never acts). (10) **Low-balance warning before bills hit**: "After Friday's bills you'll have £86 until payday." (11) **Refunds tracker**: refunds expected from returns/cancellations that haven't arrived after N days, with a chase drafted. (12) **Net worth**: linked accounts + manual entries (pension, savings elsewhere, car, debts) + trading value from Kestrel's event channel; monthly line chart. (13) **Side hustle and tax**: business income/expenses tagged (business Gmail invoices + its bank account), a "set aside for tax" suggestion as a % he chooses, Self Assessment dates (with UK money deadlines) and a year-end export for an accountant; never files anything. (14) **Shared with his wife**: tag shared costs, a running "who paid what" split, optional shared monthly budget on the family page (only what he chooses to share). (15) **Challenges**: no-spend days, a weekly "fun money" allowance, a category cap with a progress bar; streaks in Goals. (16) **Ask/Talk**: "how much did I spend on takeaways last month?", "what's coming out this week?", "can I afford £300 on Saturday?" (answered from his own data, with the forecast). (17) **Travel**: abroad, a tip on which linked card has no foreign fees. (18) **Year in review** each January; one-tap **Delete all money data** and Disconnect per bank. **History comes from the banks themselves**: on first consent request the longest history each bank allows (typically up to 12–24 months; some only 90 days) and backfill once; no export from his old app needed. Meka answered 2026-10-12 21:21: Enable Banking OK; he will link four providers (a mix of charge/credit cards, a current account and an e-money account) — he picks them in Connect bank; do not name them in the repo (rule 13). Check each is covered by Enable Banking's UK list in slice 1 and say clearly in the app if one isn't (fallback for that one: import its CSV statement).

- [~] **Trading tracker — Hyperliquid, read-only by public address (Meka, 2026-10-12 21:23: "track whether I'm making money or not, all automated").** Hard constraint honoured: **no keys, no API wallet, no signing, nothing that can trade** — Hyperliquid account data is public, so the server reads it from the public Info API (`POST https://api.hyperliquid.xyz/info`: clearinghouseState, userFills/userFillsByTime, userFunding, userNonFundingLedgerUpdates for deposits/withdrawals, portfolio) with only his **public wallet address(es)** (main + any sub-accounts he adds), entered on the device and stored in his encrypted data — never in the repo (rule 13). Polled every few minutes while positions are open, hourly otherwise. Shows (Money → Trading, and a Today/brief line): equity now and its curve; P&L today/week/month/all-time split into **realised, unrealised, fees and funding**, with **deposits/withdrawals kept out of P&L** ("you're up £X on £Y put in"); open positions with size, entry, PnL and **distance to liquidation**; win rate, average win/loss, best/worst trade, per coin; per sub-account (so each Kestrel bot/strategy can be told apart if they run in separate sub-accounts). Alerts (Heads-up, his thresholds): drawdown from peak beyond X%, liquidation within Y%, a big fill, funding costs above Z/day. Feeds the money manager's net worth and the side-hustle what-if (actual trading income). Monthly trading review (facts only, no trading advice) and a CSV export for tax. Kestrel's paper results can sit alongside, labelled Paper, from its existing event channel. Tests with invented fills (no real address in fixtures).
